- sfind-f81551a4e1a8489ab102d48bae031f26: PORTUPDT applies update-file values to master with no validity checks [medium/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    Values from UPDTFILE are moved straight into the master record without type, range or domain checks, then REWRITTEN.
    
    ##### Applicability
    SI-10 requires validity checks on information inputs. The batch update file is an external input to the authoritative portfolio master.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Line 134: a 50-byte `UPDT-NEW-VALUE` is truncated into one-byte `PORT-STATUS` with no check against the A/C/S 88-levels.
    - Line 136: client name is copied without a blank or content check.
    - Lines 138-139: an alphanumeric value is MOVEd to numeric fields with no `IS NUMERIC` test, which can store corrupt values or raise a data exception on COMP-3 `PORT-TOTAL-VALUE`.
    - Lines 132-142: an action code outside S/V/N falls through the EVALUATE (no WHEN OTHER) and still REWRITEs.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Invalid statuses, blank names or corrupt valuations can be persisted to the master file used by online inquiry and reporting.
  Recommendation: Validate each update before applying it: status must be one of the PORTFLIO 88-level values, the name must be non-blank, the value must pass an `IS NUMERIC`/`FUNCTION TEST-NUMVAL` check and a range check before conversion, and the action code must be valid (add WHEN OTHER). Reject and count invalid records without REWRITE.

- sfind-e98eea4b311a4925abc132422f575797: SECMGR AUDITLOG insert omits event outcome and accessed object [medium/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    `P300-LOG-ACCESS` inserts timestamp, user, terminal, transaction, program and access type into AUDITLOG. It records no event outcome (success/failure) and no identity of the object accessed (account number). Because it is only called after a grant, it also cannot tell success from failure.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-3 requires audit records to establish event type, time, location, source, outcome, and the identity of the individuals or subjects/objects involved. SECMGR writes the only online access audit record for the inquiry transaction.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 117-125: the insert column list has no status/outcome column and no account or portfolio key. `PROGRAM` takes `SEC-RESOURCE-NAME`, which the caller always sets to the constant 'INQONLN'.
    - The shared audit layout `src/copybook/common/AUDITLOG.cpy` already defines `AUD-STATUS` (line 27) and `AUD-ACCOUNT-NO` (line 33), and this insert does not fill them.
    
    ##### Impact
    - AUDITLOG entries cannot show which client account was viewed or whether access was granted.
    - Assumption: the DB2 AUDITLOG table has, or can be given, status and key columns. The DDL was not part of this batch.
  Recommendation: Add outcome (SUCC/FAIL plus reason) and the accessed account/portfolio key to the SECMGR request area and AUDITLOG insert. Have callers log every decision, not only grants.

- sfind-e5432daeb9684cf3ae190a4295d52806: INQPORT returns positions for any COMMAREA account number without ownership check [high/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    INQPORT reads POSFILE using the account number from the COMMAREA, which INQONLN fills straight from the terminal map, and sends the record to the screen. Nothing checks whether the signed-on user may see that account. The only application authorization in the flow (SECMGR) works at program level (`INQONLN`/`READ`) and runs after INQPORT, per the INQONLN finding.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires access to information to follow approved authorizations, and AC-6 requires access limited to what the user needs. Client portfolio positions are per-account data, and INQPORT is the component that reads and discloses them. Dataset-level ESM security (outside the repo) cannot restrict by account within one VSAM file.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Line 59: the COMMAREA is copied in as-is.
    - Lines 68-82: `WS-COMMAREA-ACCOUNT-NO` becomes the RIDFLD for `READ FILE('POSFILE')`. There is no format check and no lookup of the user's entitlements.
    - Lines 86-92: the record is sent to the terminal.
    - SECMGR lines 78-101 authorize on `RESOURCE` (constant 'INQONLN') only, never on the account.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Any user who can run PINQ can view another client's positions (units, cost basis, market value) by entering that client's account number.
    - Assumption: no account-level entitlement source outside the repo is consulted. Nothing in the repo references one.
  Recommendation: Before the POSFILE read, validate the account number's format, then authorize the signed-on user (EXEC CICS ASSIGN USERID) for that specific account. Do this through a SECMGR 'A' request with the account as the resource, an entitlement table, or EXEC CICS QUERY SECURITY on an account-level profile. Return a generic not-authorized message on denial and log the decision.

- sfind-a139bc1ca7ce498c8dd21d288baf7934: Denied or failed inquiry access attempts are never written to AUDITLOG [medium/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    INQONLN asks SECMGR to write an access record (`SEC-REQUEST-TYPE 'L'`) only when both validation and authorization succeed. Denied and failed attempts leave no audit record. When the audit write itself fails, the data has already been displayed, and the only response is a warning-level error routine and RETURN.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2/AU-12 call for audit records of the defined event types, and access-control decisions (successful and unsuccessful) are standard ones. AU-5 requires a response to audit logging failures. INQONLN is the code that decides which online access events get recorded.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 151-169: the `'L'` log request sits inside two nested `IF SEC-RESPONSE-CODE = 0` blocks. A non-zero result from validate or authorize skips logging entirely.
    - Lines 82-88: the only handling for a non-zero code, including SECMGR's 'Audit logging failed' (SECMGR lines 127-133), is `P900-ERROR-ROUTINE` (ERRHNDL with `ERR-WARNING`) plus RETURN, after the inquiry output was sent.
    
    ##### Impact
    - No record exists of unauthorized attempts to view portfolio or history data.
    - An audit-write failure does not stop disclosure and raises no alert beyond a warning.
    - Not verified: whether ERRHNDL's error log is reviewed or alerted on.
  Recommendation: Call SECMGR 'L' on every decision, passing an outcome indicator (granted, denied validation, denied authorization) so AUDITLOG records failed attempts too. Do the log write before the inquiry, and when the audit write fails, deny the inquiry and raise an abend/critical condition through ERRHNDL instead of a warning.

- sfind-901c09358c29478aafa7d2cf1210dafb: SECMGR authorization stores COUNT(*) into SQLCA group and compares group [medium/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    `P200-CHECK-AUTH` selects `COUNT(*)` INTO `:WS-DB2-AREA`, the 01-level group that holds the SQLCA, and then makes the grant decision with `IF WS-DB2-AREA > 0`. That is an alphanumeric group comparison, not a test of an integer count. The access decision does not reliably reflect AUTHFILE contents and may fail open. The exact runtime result depends on precompiler/compiler handling, which I did not run.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires enforcement of approved authorizations. SECMGR is the application's central access-decision routine for the online inquiry programs.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 15-16: `WS-DB2-AREA` contains only `INCLUDE SQLCA`.
    - Lines 79-86: the COUNT is written into that group, so the SQLCA itself is overlaid.
    - Lines 88-101: `WS-DB2-AREA > 0` compares the group, which starts with SQLCAID text, against zero as alphanumeric. Non-blank text compares greater than '0', so any `SQLCODE = 0` result can come out as a grant whatever the count is.
    - Lines 42-52: the EVALUATE has no `WHEN OTHER`, so an unrecognised request type returns with whatever `SEC-RESPONSE-CODE` the caller passed in (INQONLN never initializes it).
    
    ##### Impact
    - Users with no AUTHFILE row may be authorized for `INQONLN READ`.
    - Unknown request types are not denied by default.
    - Not verified: compiled behaviour. Validation should test a user absent from AUTHFILE.
  Recommendation: Declare a dedicated `WS-AUTH-COUNT PIC S9(9) COMP` host variable, select COUNT(*) INTO it, and grant only when `SQLCODE = 0 AND WS-AUTH-COUNT > 0`. Add `WHEN OTHER` that sets a deny response code, and initialize SEC-RESPONSE-CODE to non-zero at entry so all paths fail closed.

- sfind-7c1888c0bf3e452498426dbebda5bfca: PORTUPDT DISPLAYs portfolio key with client account number to SYSOUT [low/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    On not-found and rewrite-failure, PORTUPDT DISPLAYs `PORT-KEY` (portfolio ID plus 10-digit client account number) to SYSOUT, which the JCL routes to `SYSOUT=*` with `MSGCLASS=X`. Whether that output class is access-restricted and how long it is kept is set by JES/ESM configuration outside the repo.
    
    ##### Applicability
    SI-11 requires error messages to reveal sensitive information only to authorized personnel, and SI-12 covers retention of system output. Account numbers are client financial identifiers.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 127 and 148: `PORT-KEY` is DISPLAYed in error messages.
    - JCL lines 8 and 14-16 route the output to SYSOUT.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Client account numbers appear in job output viewable by anyone with access to that JES output class.
    - Not verified: JES output class protection and spool retention.
  Recommendation: DISPLAY only the portfolio ID (or a masked account number) and the input record sequence number, and write full keys only to a protected error/audit dataset.

- sfind-7290cec8edbd46bb958838ab73cf5b66: INQONLN dispatches portfolio and history inquiries before running SECMGR checks [high/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    In each loop of `P100-PROCESS-REQUEST`, INQONLN runs the requested function (LINK to INQPORT/INQHIST, which read and SEND position/history data) and only afterwards calls `P050-SECURITY-CHECK`. The SECMGR validate/authorize decision therefore cannot stop the disclosure it is meant to gate. Any CICS transaction-level security on PINQ (RACF/ESM, outside this repo) is not visible here.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires that approved authorizations are enforced before logical access to information is granted. INQONLN is the only entry program of transaction PINQ (`src/cics/PORTDFN.csd`) and is where the application's own authorization (SECMGR resource `INQONLN`/`READ`) is invoked. Signon and transaction-attach security are handled by CICS/ESM configuration outside the repo.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 62-77: the EVALUATE on `WS-COMMAREA-FUNCTION` performs `P300-PORTFOLIO-INQUIRY` / `P400-HISTORY-INQUIRY` right away.
    - Lines 79-88: `P050-SECURITY-CHECK` runs only after that, and a non-zero `SEC-RESPONSE-CODE` causes a RETURN once the data has already been sent.
    - Lines 101-117: the LINKs pass the terminal-supplied COMMAREA (account number) to INQPORT/INQHIST. INQPORT sends the position map at its lines 86-92.
    
    ```
    RECEIVE MAP --> LINK INQPORT/INQHIST --> SEND MAP (data shown) --> SECMGR V/A --> deny => RETURN
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    - Portfolio positions and transaction history for any account number typed on the screen are shown to users who would fail the SECMGR `AUTHFILE` check.
    - The application-level access decision has no effect on disclosure. Only ESM transaction security (not verifiable here) gates PINQ.
    - Assumption: INQPORT/INQHIST do no checks of their own. I confirmed this for INQPORT, and INQHIST has no SECMGR call.
  Recommendation: Run P050-SECURITY-CHECK right after RECEIVE MAP and before the EVALUATE dispatch. Perform the INQP/INQH branches only when validate and authorize both return 0, and on denial send a generic error map and stop without LINKing to INQPORT/INQHIST. Initialize WS-SECURITY-REQUEST (including SEC-RESPONSE-CODE) before each check.

- sfind-11f0ed3759764d2f8950c1672c79ed65: PORTMSTR create and delete leave no audit record; update audit broken [high/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:09:49 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTMSTR creates (`WRITE`), updates (`REWRITE`) and deletes (`DELETE`) portfolio master records. Only the update path calls the audit routine. `2100-LOG-PORTFOLIO-UPDATE` refers to data items PORTMSTR never defines and records no real before-image, and the AUDPROC return code is ignored.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2/AU-12 require audit records for the defined events. Create, update and delete of the authoritative portfolio master are the core audited events in this system (the AUDITLOG layout defines CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE actions). AU-3 requires the record content, and AU-5 requires a response when logging fails.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 115-136 (create, `WRITE` at 126) and 209-226 (delete, `DELETE` at 215) contain no `CALL 'AUDPROC'`.
    - Line 206: update performs `2100-LOG-PORTFOLIO-UPDATE`. In that paragraph (lines 268-288), `LS-AUDIT-REQUEST`, `USERID`, `TERMINAL-ID`, `WS-BEFORE-IMAGE`, `PORT-ACCOUNT-NO`, `PORT-RECORD` and the `LS-*` targets are not declared in PORTMSTR's WORKING-STORAGE or LINKAGE (lines 41-80). No before-image is captured before the `REWRITE` at 194.
    - Line 287: the call result is never checked.
    - Lines 243-263 reuse the `2100-` paragraph prefix with further undefined items, which suggests template code that was never integrated.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Portfolio creation and deletion, including irreversible removal of master records, leave no trace of who did what.
    - Updates cannot produce a valid before/after record, and failed audit writes go unnoticed.
    - Assumption: callers of PORTMSTR do not write their own audit records. None was found in this batch.
  Recommendation: Declare the AUDPROC request area (matching AUDPROC's LS-AUDIT-REQUEST) and a before-image buffer in PORTMSTR. READ the existing record before REWRITE/DELETE to capture the before-image, obtain the user and terminal IDs from the caller or the execution environment, and call AUDPROC with action CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE and SUCC/FAIL status on every mutation path, failures included. Check the AUDPROC return code and fail the operation (or raise a critical error) when the audit write fails.

  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTUPDT applies sequential update transactions (status, client name, total value) to the portfolio master with `REWRITE`. It writes no audit record and does not update the record's own `PORT-LAST-USER`/`PORT-LAST-TRANS` fields, so each change leaves no who/what/before/after trace.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2/AU-12 require audit records for the defined event types, and AU-3 requires their content. Changes to the authoritative portfolio master, including client name and holdings value, are audited event types per the AUDITLOG UPDATE action, and AUDPROC exists for this purpose.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 131-150: `2200-APPLY-UPDATE` overwrites fields and `REWRITE`s (line 142) with no AUDPROC call and no before-image saved.
    - The only output is SYSOUT counters (lines 156-157).
    - The JCL `src/jcl/portfolio/PORTUPDT.jcl` allocates no audit dataset.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Bulk changes to client names, account status and valuations cannot be traced to a job, a user, or prior values.
    - Assumption: no external SMF/ESM logging is relied on for record-level change history. That would record dataset access only, not field changes.
  Recommendation: Before each REWRITE, copy the read record to a before-image. After the REWRITE, call AUDPROC with system/user (job or ESM user), program PORTUPDT, action UPDATE, SUCC/FAIL status, the PORT-KEY, and the before/after images.   Description: ##### Overview

- sfind-e8d49607288346298e3b47c5d8b48e8b: RCVPRC00 recovery resets completed jobs and restarts without limit [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:52 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    Recovery rewrites batch-control status for each selected process without checking its current state, so 'S'/'A' recovery resets DONE processes to READY or forces DONE on failed ones; restartable processes are restarted with no max-restart check. No program currently CALLs RCVPRC00.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-24 (fail to a known state preserving state information) and SI-7 (information integrity) apply to the operator recovery handler that changes batch-control state driving which jobs PRCSEQ00 runs next.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    Lines 159-171: 2100-RECOVER-PROCESS reads the record and acts without testing BCT-STATUS. Lines 182-190: the BCT-MAX-RESTARTS test runs only for non-restartable processes; restartable ones always restart. Lines 217-227: bypass marks a process DONE with RC 4, which PRCSEQ00 2210-CHECK-DEP-STATUS treats as satisfied when PSR-DEP-RC >= 4. Lines 263-281: recover-all walks every record regardless of date.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Completed jobs can be re-queued and rerun, duplicating postings; failed jobs can be bypassed so dependents run on incomplete data.
    - Unbounded restart loops for restartable jobs.
    - Not verified: how operators invoke RCVPRC00 (no caller in repo).
  Recommendation: Only act on processes in ERROR/ACTIVE (abended) state, apply BCT-MAX-RESTARTS to all restarts, require an explicit operator flag for bypass and record it, and restrict recover-all to the requested process date.

- sfind-c9a8e4bea47d4d5db222680f5dfdffd7: AUDPROC never sets RETURN-CODE; PORTTRAN cannot detect audit write failure [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:52 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    AUDPROC reports open/write failures only in LS-RETURN-CODE inside the caller's parameter block and via DISPLAY; PORTTRAN tests the RETURN-CODE special register, which AUDPROC never sets, so failed audit writes for portfolio transactions are treated as success.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    AU-5 requires alerting and defined action on audit logging failure; AU-12 requires records to be generated for audited events. AUDPROC is the shared audit writer for PORTTRAN and PORTMSTR.
    
    ##### Evidence

    ```
    AUDFILE open/write fails --> LS-RETURN-CODE=8 + DISPLAY --> PORTTRAN checks RETURN-CODE (0) --> transaction continues unaudited
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Portfolio transactions can be applied with no audit record and no alert.
    - Not verified: PORTMSTR's post-call check (other batch).
  Recommendation: Set RETURN-CODE as well as LS-RETURN-CODE on failure (or change callers to test the returned field), make callers stop the transaction on audit failure, and route the failure to an operator alert (e.g. WTO/console message) rather than DISPLAY only.

  Description: ##### Overview
    
    All callers in this batch pass ERR-MESSAGE (ERRHAND copybook, beginning with an 18-byte timestamp), but ERRPROC's LS-ERROR-REQUEST begins with an 8-byte program ID, so every error-log record has shifted program, category, code, severity and text fields.

    AU-3 requires event records to establish what, when, where (source) and outcome; ERRPROC writes the system's sequential error log ERRLOG.

- sfind-a52a42cd7cc8492f83c658f50e2715bd: CKPRST checkpoint/restart entry points are empty stubs [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:52 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    CKPRST accepts INIT/TAKE/COMMIT/RESTART requests but every paragraph contains only a comment, so no checkpoint is recorded, verified or restored and RETURN-STATUS is never set. No program in the repo currently CALLs CKPRST, which bounds present exposure.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-24 (fail in a known state preserving state information) and SI-7/SI-7(1) (integrity checks of information) apply to the shared checkpoint/restart service the batch design relies on to resume interrupted jobs without lost or duplicate records.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    Lines 43-57: PROC-INIT, PROC-TAKE-CHECKPOINT, PROC-COMMIT-CHECKPOINT and PROC-RESTART have no statements (the `*` lines in Area B are not valid comment indicators either). The CHECKPOINT-FILE declared at lines 7-12 is never opened, read or written, and RETURN-STATUS is returned unchanged.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - A caller would receive an unchanged status and believe a checkpoint was taken or a restart position restored.
    - No integrity check (hash, counts reconciliation) over checkpoint data exists.
    - Not verified: whether any JCL or future program depends on CKPRST; no CALL was found in src/programs.
  Recommendation: Implement the checkpoint record write/read against CKPTFILE with file-status checks, store record counts and a key/position plus an integrity value (e.g. a hash of the checkpointed counters), verify it on RESTART, and set RETURN-STATUS to a failure code on any error so callers stop rather than continue.

- sfind-7420aa16f47949199d4c94a7027ab811: HISTLD00 commits without SQLCODE checks and writes checkpoint after commit [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:52 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    HISTLD00 issues COMMIT WORK at the threshold and at termination without checking SQLCODE, then rewrites the VSAM checkpoint counters as if the commit succeeded; restart logic never reads those counters back.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-24 and SI-7 apply to this batch load of position history into DB2 POSHIST, which must commit DB2 work and record its VSAM checkpoint consistently and fail to a known state.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    Lines 180-187 and 203-207: COMMIT is followed unconditionally by 2310-UPDATE-CHECKPOINT; a failed commit is recorded as progress. Line 130-131: the control record REWRITE has no INVALID KEY/status check. 9000-ERROR-ROUTINE (lines 226-232) rolls back but returns to the processing loop, so after a rollback the in-memory WS-RECORDS-WRITTEN still includes rolled-back rows and the next checkpoint persists them. 1300-INIT-CHECKPOINTS (lines 120-132) never uses BCT-RECORDS-READ to reposition on restart; duplicates on rerun are silently skipped by the SQLCODE -803 CONTINUE at line 168-169.
    
    ```
    COMMIT (rc unchecked) --> REWRITE checkpoint counts --> restart ignores counts
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Batch control counts can overstate what was durably loaded into POSHIST, losing reliable state information for recovery.
    - -803 suppression masks reprocessing but also masks genuine key collisions.
    - Not verified: DB2-ERROR-ROUTINE in DBPROC behaviour beyond its ROLLBACK/ERRPROC call.
  Recommendation: Test SQLCODE after each COMMIT and terminate (with ROLLBACK and a non-zero RC) on failure; only rewrite the checkpoint after a successful commit and check its file status; reset counters to the last committed values on rollback; on restart, read BCT-RECORDS-READ and skip that many input records; count -803 occurrences separately and report them.

    ##### Applicability

- sfind-308214267a3d4fde8115593d5fd895b8: DB2CMT passes LS-ERROR-INFO to DB2ERR, so commit/rollback failures go unlogged [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:52 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    On commit, rollback or savepoint failure DB2CMT calls DB2ERR with only the SQLCODE/message area, but DB2ERR expects a request beginning with a 4-byte function code; the SQLCODE bytes are read as the function, DB2ERR falls to 'Invalid function code', and no ERRLOG row is written. Rollback failure is returned as RC 8 without failing closed. No program currently CALLs DB2CMT.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-24 (fail to a known state) and AU-12/AU-5 (generate records and respond to logging failures) apply to the shared DB2 commit controller.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    Line 169 `CALL 'DB2ERR' USING LS-ERROR-INFO` passes the 84-byte area defined at lines 48-50; DB2ERR's LS-ERROR-REQUEST (DB2ERR lines 39-53) starts with LS-FUNCTION X(4) and LS-PROGRAM-ID, so FUNC-LOG never matches. Lines 103-117: rollback failure only sets RC 8.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - DB2 commit/rollback failures leave no ERRLOG record with SQLCODE/SQLSTATE.
    - A failed rollback leaves the unit of work in an undefined state while the caller may continue.
  Recommendation: Build a DB2ERR request (function 'LOG ', program 'DB2CMT', SQLCODE, SQLSTATE, text) and pass it; on rollback failure return a terminal code (16) and document that callers must abend.

- sfind-f3e555274cc2451b9f62f068c032010d: SECMGR user validation compares caller-supplied ID to same CICS USERID [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:40 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    P100-VALIDATE-USER only checks that the user ID in the COMMAREA equals the ID returned by EXEC CICS ASSIGN USERID. The only caller in the repo, INQONLN, fills that COMMAREA field from the same ASSIGN USERID, so the check always passes. SECMGR adds no authentication of its own: no ESM/RACF verification, no counter of failed attempts, no lockout. Whether CICS sign-on is enforced by the region's ESM is runtime configuration outside the repo.
    
    ##### Applicability
    IA-2 covers identifying and authenticating organizational users. AC-7 covers limiting consecutive invalid logon attempts and the action taken when the limit is hit. SECMGR is the documented security manager for online programs (lines 4-8), so this is where application-level validation and attempt limiting would sit. Sign-on done by CICS/RACF before the transaction starts is out of scope for this repo and must be evidenced from the region's SIT/ESM settings.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - SECMGR.cbl:56-74 compares `SEC-USER-ID` to `WS-USER-ID`, both taken from ASSIGN USERID.
    - INQONLN.cbl:142-144 sets `SEC-USER-ID` from ASSIGN USERID right before the LINK.
    - So the comparison is tautological. A failure (code 8) cannot happen through the in-repo caller, and no failed-attempt state is kept or checked anywhere.
    
    ##### Impact
    - The 'Validates CICS user credentials' function documented in the header does no credential validation. Identity rests entirely on whatever the region provides, which could be the default CICS user if sign-on is not enforced.
    - No [number] of invalid attempts and no lock action is implemented in the application.
    - Not verified: CICS SEC=YES / RACF sign-on settings and the default-user configuration of the region.
  Recommendation: Replace the tautological comparison with a real ESM-backed check: EXEC CICS VERIFY PASSWORD/QUERY SECURITY, or rely on CICS sign-on and reject the region default user ID. Persist failed validation and authorization attempts per user and lock or deny after an organization-defined threshold.
  Code owners: Sergio DuBois <sergio.dubois@gmail.com>

- sfind-e434e488f3df422eb607fbdf6650e294: SECMGR audit omits outcome and never records denied validation or authorization [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:40 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    SECMGR writes AUDITLOG rows only when asked with request type 'L'. INQONLN asks for that only after validation and authorization both succeed. Neither P100 nor P200 writes a record when it denies access, and the AUDITLOG insert has no outcome or status column.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2 and AU-12 cover generating records for the defined security event types, which include logon and access-denial events. AU-3 requires records to show the outcome of the event. AC-2(12) monitoring of atypical usage depends on failed attempts being recorded. SECMGR is the component documented as maintaining the security audit trail (line 8).
    
    ##### Evidence
    - SECMGR.cbl:62-74 and 88-101 set failure codes and messages, but no audit write follows.
    - SECMGR.cbl:117-125 inserts timestamp, user, terminal, transaction, program and access type, with no outcome field.
    - INQONLN.cbl:161-168 links with 'L' only inside the success branches.
    
    ```
    P100/P200 failure --code 8/12--> caller --no 'L' request--> no AUDITLOG row
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    - Failed and denied access attempts leave no application audit trail, so atypical usage and brute-force attempts cannot be detected from AUDITLOG.
    - Successful records do not state their outcome explicitly. It can only be inferred from the fact that a row exists.
    - Not verified: whether CICS/RACF SMF records capture these denials at the platform level.
  Recommendation: Have P100 and P200 call P300 (or an equivalent insert) on every outcome, and add an OUTCOME/STATUS column plus the reason code to the AUDITLOG insert, so that granted, denied and failed events are all recorded.

- sfind-d0ef075d1daf4126a5edf412faff43c0: PORTADD creates portfolio master records with no audit record [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:40 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTADD writes new records to the portfolio master VSAM file and produces only count totals on SYSOUT. It does not call AUDPROC, does not write an audit file, and does not set PORT-LAST-USER, so nothing records who created which portfolio or when.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2 and AU-12 cover generating audit records for defined events, and record creation in the system of record is one of them. AU-3 covers the identity, outcome and object content of those records. The repo provides AUDPROC and the AUDITLOG copybook for this purpose, and sibling programs (PORTDEL, PORTTRAN) use them.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - PORTADD.cbl:113-137 validates, sets the create and maintenance dates, and issues WRITE PORT-RECORD. Successes, duplicates and errors only increment counters or DISPLAY the ID.
    - No AUDIT file is selected (lines 20-31), and there is no CALL 'AUDPROC' anywhere in the program.
    - PORT-LAST-USER (PORTFLIO.cpy line 32) is never populated.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Creation of client portfolio records (account number, client name, financial data) cannot be traced to an individual or a job run.
    - Assumption: the JCL job log and SMF are not treated as the application audit trail. SMF job records would show the submitter but not which records were created.
  Recommendation: After each WRITE attempt, populate an AUDITLOG AUDIT-RECORD (user ID, program, action CREATE, SUCC/FAIL status, portfolio ID and account number, after-image) and CALL 'AUDPROC'. Set PORT-LAST-USER before the WRITE. Stop or flag the run if AUDPROC returns non-zero.

                     MOVE VAL-ERR-ID TO LS-ERROR-MSG

            * Account number must be 10 numeric digits
            *----------------------------------------------------------------*

                 OR LS-INPUT-VALUE = ZEROS

                     MOVE VAL-ERR-ACCT TO LS-ERROR-MSG
                     EXIT PARAGRAPH

                 MOVE SPACES TO LS-ERROR-MSG

                     MOVE VAL-ERR-TYPE TO LS-ERROR-MSG
                     EXIT PARAGRAPH
                 END-IF
                 
                 MOVE VAL-SUCCESS TO LS-RETURN-CODE
                 MOVE SPACES TO LS-ERROR-MSG
                 .

            * Amount must be within valid range
            *----------------------------------------------------------------*
                 MOVE LS-INPUT-VALUE TO VAL-TEMP-NUM

                 OR VAL-TEMP-NUM > VAL-MAX-AMOUNT
                     MOVE VAL-INVALID-AMT TO LS-RETURN-CODE
                     MOVE VAL-ERR-AMT TO LS-ERROR-MSG

    The deletion audit timestamp comes from `ACCEPT ... FROM TIME STAMP`. That is not a standard or IBM Enterprise COBOL ACCEPT source, so the value's format, and whether it carries any UTC offset, is undefined in the code.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-8 requires audit timestamps generated from the system clock in UTC, or with a fixed offset or mapping, at a defined granularity. AUD-TIMESTAMP is the only time field in the PORTDEL audit record.
    
    ##### Evidence

    ##### Impact
    - Deletion audit records may have no timestamp that can be interpreted or correlated with other records. Behavior depends on the compiler: it may be rejected at compile time or yield local time with no zone.
    - Not verified: the compiler and runtime behavior.
  Recommendation: Use MOVE FUNCTION CURRENT-DATE TO WS-TIMESTAMP (21 characters including the GMT offset), or a DB2 CURRENT TIMESTAMP with an explicit UTC conversion, and document the format.

- sfind-27a12148783f423e91a65aa6ed68e21f: SECMGR authorization can fail open on unknown request type and SQLCA-bound COUNT [medium/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:40 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    SECMGR's access decision has two fail-open paths. First, the request-type EVALUATE has no WHEN OTHER, so an unrecognized request type returns without setting SEC-RESPONSE-CODE and the caller's previous value (0 after a successful call) is left in place. Second, the P200 COUNT(*) is selected INTO WS-DB2-AREA, which is the group holding the SQLCA, rather than a numeric host variable. The `> 0` test therefore compares the SQLCA bytes, not a count. How the precompiler or runtime handles the second path was not verified.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires that approved authorizations are enforced for logical access. SC-24 requires failing to a known state. SECMGR's P200 is the application's authorization enforcement point for online resources.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - SECMGR.cbl:42-52 dispatches only on 'V', 'A' and 'L', with no default branch that sets a denial code.
    - SECMGR.cbl:15-16 makes WS-DB2-AREA the SQLCA include group.
    - SECMGR.cbl:79-96 uses WS-DB2-AREA as the INTO target and the comparison operand. If the decision is made on SQLCA bytes such as the 'SQLCA' eyecatcher, the result is not driven by the AUTHFILE row count.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Access to online resources could be granted with no matching AUTHFILE entry.
    - Assumption: the code compiles as written. If the precompiler rejects line 81, P200 is not deployable at all.
    - Runtime verification is needed: call P200 for a user with no AUTHFILE row and check the response code.
  Recommendation: Add WHEN OTHER that sets a denial code (e.g. 8 with 'Invalid security request'), and initialize SEC-RESPONSE-CODE to a non-zero value on entry. Select COUNT(*) into a dedicated PIC S9(9) COMP host variable and grant only when SQLCODE = 0 and that count > 0.
  Note: Duplicate of sfind-ebd8f670cd7b47be83adb0c215e5a38f.
  Scan session: 0f683593048c40b6828dc363f5984ec6
  Snippet: src/programs/online/SECMGR.cbl:42-54
    Commentary: There is no WHEN OTHER, so an unknown request type returns with SEC-RESPONSE-CODE unchanged.
    Code:
                 EVALUATE TRUE
                     WHEN SEC-VALIDATE
                          PERFORM P100-VALIDATE-USER
                             THRU P100-EXIT
                     WHEN SEC-AUTHORIZE
                          PERFORM P200-CHECK-AUTH
                             THRU P200-EXIT
                     WHEN SEC-AUDIT
                          PERFORM P300-LOG-ACCESS
                             THRU P300-EXIT
                 END-EVALUATE.
                 
                 EXEC CICS RETURN END-EXEC.
  Snippet: src/programs/online/SECMGR.cbl:15-16
    Commentary: WS-DB2-AREA is the group containing the SQLCA.
    Code:

- sfind-23737c7028f94e3f9a2829b83db4b3fd: PORTDEL deletion audit record lacks user identity and deleted content [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:40 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTDEL writes an audit record for each successful VSAM DELETE. The record holds only a timestamp, the action, the key, the reason code and the status byte. There is no user or job identity, no program name, and no image of the deleted record. Failed deletes and not-found requests are not audited at all.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-3 requires records to establish the identity of the individual or process, and the outcome. AU-10 relates to proving who performed a deletion. Deleting client portfolio master records is a security-relevant event that falls under AU-12.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - PORTDEL.cbl:56-62 defines AUDIT-RECORD with no user, program or before-image field. 27 bytes are FILLER.
    - PORTDEL.cbl:168-177 fills only those fields.
    - PORTDEL.cbl:146-165 has the not-found and failed-delete paths, which only DISPLAY.
    
    ##### Impact
    - A deleted portfolio, including client name and balances, cannot be reconstructed from the audit trail, and the deletion cannot be attributed to a person.
    - Only successful deletes appear in the trail. Failed or unauthorized attempts are visible only on SYSOUT.
  Recommendation: Switch to the shared AUDITLOG copybook and AUDPROC. Record the user/job ID, the program, action DELETE, SUCC/FAIL, the portfolio ID and account, and the record image as it was before deletion. Audit failed and not-found delete requests as well.

- sfind-0d32237d441745b299329cda99a3b7a1: PORTDEL audit write failure only DISPLAYs; deletion already committed [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:40 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTDEL deletes the master record first and writes the audit record afterwards. If the audit WRITE fails, the program DISPLAYs a message and moves on to the next deletion with the return code unchanged, so the job can end RC=0 having deleted records that were never audited.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-5 requires alerting and defined actions when audit logging fails. PORTDEL's audit file is the only record of portfolio deletions.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - PORTDEL.cbl:157-166 performs DELETE and then PERFORM 2300-WRITE-AUDIT.
    - PORTDEL.cbl:179-181 only DISPLAYs on a non-'00' audit status, and WS-RETURN-CODE is not set.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Portfolio deletions can be lost from the audit trail without anyone being alerted or the job failing.
    - Not verified: whether operations monitor SYSOUT for this message.
  Recommendation: On audit write failure, set a non-zero return code, stop processing further deletions, and issue an operator/console alert. Where possible, write the audit record before committing the delete, or roll back the delete.

  Description: ##### Overview
    SECMGR P200 decides access by counting AUTHFILE rows for SEC-USER-ID taken from the caller's COMMAREA, not the CICS-signed-on user, and the COUNT result is selected into the SQLCA group rather than a numeric host variable, so the access test at line 90 does not reliably evaluate the count. No ESM (RACF QUERY SECURITY) call is made. AUTHFILE has no DDL in the repo.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 (enforce approved authorizations) and AC-6 (least privilege): SECMGR is the repo's central online access-enforcement function. Any program that can LINK SECMGR controls the identity being authorized.
    
    ##### Evidence
    Lines 79-86 use `:SEC-USER-ID` (linkage, line 35) in the WHERE clause; the trusted ASSIGN USERID is obtained only in P100/P300. Line 81 selects INTO `:WS-DB2-AREA`, which is the SQLCA include group (lines 15-16), and line 90 compares that group to 0. P100 (lines 57-74) only checks that the supplied ID equals ASSIGN USERID and INQONLN fills SEC-USER-ID from the same ASSIGN, so P100 never adds an authentication factor.
    
    ##### Impact
    - A caller can request authorization for another user's ID; the authorization result may not reflect AUTHFILE content at all.
    - Authorization data (AUTHFILE) is unmanaged in-repo: no DDL, no grants, no maintenance path.
    - Not verified: compile behaviour of the SQLCA host-variable usage and whether RACF resource checks exist at runtime.
  Recommendation: In P200, obtain the user via EXEC CICS ASSIGN USERID (ignore the COMMAREA value) and use EXEC CICS QUERY SECURITY RESTYPE/RESID or a RACF profile for the resource; if AUTHFILE is kept, select COUNT(*) into a dedicated PIC S9(9) COMP host variable, treat any SQL error as deny, and add AUTHFILE DDL and grants to the repo.

- sfind-bef018b2d0b6403bbb8d2437a7c697ec: INQONLN dispatches inquiry LINKs before running the SECMGR authorization check [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:30 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    INQONLN's main loop runs the requested inquiry (INQPORT / INQHIST LINK, which sends portfolio data to the terminal) and only afterwards runs P050-SECURITY-CHECK, so the authorization decision cannot stop the data access it is meant to guard. Whether CICS transaction-level security (RACF TCICSTRN for PINQ) is active at runtime is outside the repo and was not verified.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires approved authorizations to be enforced for logical access; SECMGR is the repo's application-level access enforcement for the PINQ transaction, so the order in which INQONLN calls it decides whether it enforces anything. CICS/RACF transaction security is a separate layer whose evidence must come from the ESM configuration.
    
    ##### Evidence
    Lines 62-77 evaluate WS-COMMAREA-FUNCTION and PERFORM P300/P400, which LINK INQPORT/INQHIST (lines 101-117). The security check at lines 79-80 and the denial at 82-88 come after that EVALUATE. INQHIST sends HISMAP with history rows itself, so by the time SEC-RESPONSE-CODE is non-zero the data has already been shown.
    
    ```
    RECEIVE MAP --> EVALUATE --> LINK INQHIST --> SEND MAP(history) --> P050 SECMGR check --> RETURN if denied
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    - Portfolio and transaction-history data reaches users that SECMGR/AUTHFILE would deny.
    - Assumes INQPORT behaves like INQHIST (sends its own map); INQPORT was not in this batch.
    - Not verified: whether RACF transaction/resource security restricts PINQ at runtime.
  Recommendation: Move PERFORM P050-SECURITY-CHECK (and the non-zero SEC-RESPONSE-CODE exit) to immediately after RECEIVE MAP and before the EVALUATE, and pass a per-function resource name (e.g. INQPORT/INQHIST) to SECMGR so each inquiry is authorized before it is dispatched.
  Note: Merged duplicates: sfind-d577c42c52b546d0bdc74ad2814b79a2, sfind-7290cec8edbd46bb958838ab73cf5b66. Related to SECMGR authorization group sfind-ebd8f670cd7b47be83adb0c215e5a38f.

- sfind-6d06cd4a03f74d7ebc196805ca8c2fa6: ERRHNDL ERRLOG insert mismatches table schema and omits user identity [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:30 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    ERRHNDL inserts eight positional values into ERRLOG, but the committed ERRLOG DDL defines ten NOT NULL-heavy columns with different types and order, so the insert cannot succeed against that table; the record also carries no user ID or terminal.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-12 (generate the audit records defined) and AU-3 (identity and outcome in records): ERRHNDL is the central online error/event logger. AU-5 covers what happens when logging fails.
    
    ##### Evidence
    ERRHNDL lines 73-83 `INSERT INTO ERRLOG VALUES` with timestamp, program, paragraph (X30), SQLCODE, CICS RESP, severity (X1), message, trace ID. ERRLOG.sql lines 15-25 expects ERROR_TIMESTAMP, PROGRAM_ID, ERROR_TYPE CHAR(1), ERROR_SEVERITY INTEGER, ERROR_CODE CHAR(8), ERROR_MESSAGE, PROCESS_DATE, PROCESS_TIME, USER_ID NOT NULL, ADDITIONAL_INFO.    
    ```
    online error --> ERRHNDL INSERT (8 cols) --> ERRLOG (10 cols) --> SQL error --> ERR-FATAL --> ABEND, no record
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    - Online errors (including security-check failures routed here) are never recorded in ERRLOG and every logged warning becomes an abend.
    - Even if aligned, records would lack the user identity AU-3 requires.
    - Assumes the deployed ERRLOG matches src/database/db2/ERRLOG.sql.
  Recommendation: Use an explicit column list matching ERRLOG.sql, map severity to the INTEGER scale, populate PROCESS_DATE/TIME and USER_ID (from EXEC CICS ASSIGN USERID) and use CURRENT TIMESTAMP; on insert failure write a fallback record (e.g. CSMT/TD queue) before deciding to abend.

  Description: ##### Overview
    The only security audit record written online (P300) stores timestamp, user, terminal, transaction, resource and access type, but no event outcome and no accessed object (account). The timestamp is FUNCTION CURRENT-DATE moved into a 26-byte field and inserted into a column named TIMESTAMP; AUDITLOG has no DDL in the repo, so the column type and whether the insert succeeds could not be confirmed.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-3 requires records to establish the type, outcome and subjects/objects of an event; AU-8 requires time stamps with UTC mapping. P300 is the audit-record generator for online access.
    
    ##### Evidence
    Lines 117-125 insert six columns, none carrying outcome or account number. Line 106 uses CURRENT-DATE (YYYYMMDDhhmmsshh±hhmm, 21 chars) rather than a DB2 TIMESTAMP/CURRENT TIMESTAMP value. INQONLN only calls P300 after success, so no outcome field exists to distinguish events.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Audit trail cannot show which account was viewed or whether access was granted.

                 END-IF.

- sfind-29d056bf06c4452b94e1c480a2ac6fe4: CURSMGR executes caller-supplied SQL text with no authorization or validation [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:30 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    CURSMGR accepts a 240-byte SQL statement and cursor name from its COMMAREA and declares/opens a cursor over it, with no check of who the caller is, which tables the statement touches, or what the statement contains. The program as committed is incomplete (P300/P400 paragraphs are absent and DECLARE with host-variable names is not valid static SQL), so the effect depends on how it is completed; the design intent is a generic SQL executor.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-6 / AC-3 (only authorized accesses through processes acting for users) and SI-10 (validate inputs): CURSMGR runs under the PINQ plan's DB2 authority (DB2ENTRY PORTDB2, PLAN PORTPLAN), so the statement text is the access decision.
    
    ##### Evidence
    Lines 34-35 define CURS-NAME and CURS-STMT in the linkage area; lines 70-72 declare a cursor directly from them and line 84 opens it. No SECMGR call, no allow-list, no statement check exists anywhere in the program.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Any program that can LINK CURSMGR can read any table PORTPLAN can read, bypassing SECMGR.
    - Not verified: final implementation of FETCH/CLOSE and the PORTPLAN package grants.
  Recommendation: Replace the free-form statement with a fixed set of static cursors selected by a request code (e.g. HISTORY_CURSOR with a bound ACCOUNT_NO host variable), and reject unknown codes; if dynamic SQL is retained, PREPARE only from an allow-listed statement table and authorize the caller via SECMGR first.

  Description: ##### Overview
    The 100-connection cap is enforced against WS-ACTIVE-CONNECTIONS held in WORKING-STORAGE; CICS gives each LINK a fresh copy of WORKING-STORAGE, so the counter starts at 0 every call and the cap never triggers.
    
    ##### Applicability
    SC-5 (limit effects of denial-of-service) and AC-10 (limit concurrent sessions): DB2ONLN is the repo's only connection-limiting control for online DB2 access.
    
    ##### Evidence
    Lines 20-22 initialise the counters with VALUE clauses in WORKING-STORAGE; line 55 compares against WS-MAX-CONNECTIONS; lines 70 and 95 increment/decrement a counter that is discarded at RETURN (line 52). Nothing persists it in a TS queue, CWA, or shared storage.
    
    ##### Impact
    - The intended limit on concurrent DB2 connections is not enforced; protection rests on CICS DB2ENTRY THREADLIMIT/DB2CONN settings outside this program.
    - P300 also returns this per-call counter as status, so monitoring based on it is meaningless.
    - Not verified: runtime DB2CONN/DB2ENTRY thread limits.
  Recommendation: Rely on and document CICS DB2ENTRY/DB2CONN THREADLIMIT for PORTDB2, or keep the counter in shared storage (CWA or a recoverable TS queue with ENQ) so the cap applies across tasks.

  Description: ##### Overview

    ##### Applicability
    
    AU-2/AU-12 (generate audit records for data modification events) and AC-6(9) (log privileged batch maintenance) for the portfolio master, which the AUDITLOG design (CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE with before/after images) covers.
    
    ##### Evidence

    - Assumed no external SMF/VSAM journaling substitutes; not verified.

- sfind-f71d2824824b49218679e2bd139f26bc: Diff harness reports field values and raw hex of mismatching records [low/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:21 UTC, category: observation

  Scan session: 0f683593048c40b6828dc363f5984ec6
  Snippet: python/cobol_fields/diff.py:88-108
    Commentary: decode errors caught and reported as findings instead of aborting.
    Code:
      def diff_records(layout: RecordLayout, expected: bytes, actual: bytes, record_no: int = 1,
                       *, strict: bool = False, ignore: Iterable[str] = ()) -> list[FieldDiff]:
          ignored = set(ignore)
          out: list[FieldDiff] = []
          for f in layout.data_fields:
              if f.name in ignored or f.short_name in ignored:
                  continue
              e_raw, a_raw = expected[f.offset:f.end], actual[f.offset:f.end]
              if e_raw == a_raw:
                  continue
              try:
                  e_val = f.field.decode(e_raw, strict=strict)
              except Exception as exc:  # undecodable data is itself a finding
                  e_val = f"<undecodable: {exc}>"
              try:
                  a_val = f.field.decode(a_raw, strict=strict)
              except Exception as exc:
                  a_val = f"<undecodable: {exc}>"
              out.append(FieldDiff(record_no, f.name, e_val, a_val, e_raw.hex(), a_raw.hex(),
                                   _classify(f, e_val, a_val, e_raw, a_raw)))
          return out
  Snippet: python/cobol_fields/diff.py:29-31
    Commentary: diff output includes values and hex of both records' fields.
    Code:
          def __str__(self) -> str:
              return (f"record {self.record_no} {self.field}: expected {self.expected!r} "
                      f"[{self.expected_hex}] got {self.actual!r} [{self.actual_hex}] ({self.reason})")

  Description: ##### Overview

    ##### Applicability
    
    AU-8 (audit time stamps that use UTC or have a fixed local-time offset from UTC) for the system's audit record.
    
    ##### Evidence

FROM TIME STAMP (local, no zone); PORTTRAN.cbl line 252 moves FUNCTION CURRENT-DATE (21-char YYYYMMDDhhmmssccsHHMM incl. offset) into the same 26-byte field, a different layout.

    Commentary: encode-side range and scale validation.
    Code:
                True (what COBOL silently does), otherwise ``CobolFieldError``.
              """
              d = _to_decimal(value)
              if not d.is_finite():
                  raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: non-finite value {d}")
              if self.pic.scale_shift:
                  d = d.scaleb(-self.pic.scale_shift)
              q = d.quantize(Decimal(1).scaleb(-self.scale), rounding=ROUND_DOWN)
              if q != d and not truncate:
                  raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: {d} has more than {self.scale} decimal places")
              n = int(q.scaleb(self.scale))
              if n < 0 and not self.signed:
                  if not truncate:
                      raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: negative value {d} into unsigned PIC {self.pic.raw}")
                  n = -n
              if self.usage is Usage.COMP_X:
                  limit = 256 ** self.length
                  if n >= limit or n < 0:
                      if not truncate:
                          raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: {d} exceeds COMP-X range 0..{limit - 1}")
                      n %= limit
                  return n
              if self.usage is Usage.COMP_5 or (self.usage is Usage.COMP and not self.dialect.binary_truncate):
                  bits = self.length * 8
                  lo, hi = (-(2 ** (bits - 1)), 2 ** (bits - 1) - 1) if self.signed else (0, 2 ** bits - 1)
                  if n < lo or n > hi:
                      if not truncate:
                          raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: {d} exceeds binary range {lo}..{hi}")
                      n %= 2 ** bits
                      if self.signed and n > hi:
                          n -= 2 ** bits
                  return n
              limit = 10 ** self.pic.digits
              if abs(n) >= limit:
                  if not truncate:
                      raise CobolFieldError(
                          f"{self.name}: {d} does not fit PIC {self.pic.raw} (max {self.max_value})")
                  sign = -1 if n < 0 else 1
                  n = sign * (abs(n) % limit)

      def cmd_dump(args) -> int:
          layout = load_layout(args.copybook, args.record, _dialect(args))
          data = Path(args.file).read_bytes()
          if len(data) % layout.length:
              print(f"warning: {len(data)} bytes is not a multiple of record length {layout.length}",
                    file=sys.stderr)
          records = []
          for i, raw in enumerate(iter_records(data, layout.length), 1):
              rec = layout.decode(raw, strict=not args.lenient)
              if args.json:
                  records.append(rec)
              else:
                  print(f"--- record {i}")
                  for k, v in rec.items():
                      shown = repr(v) if isinstance(v, str) else v
                      print(f"  {k:<40} {shown}")
          if args.json:
              json.dump(records, sys.stdout, default=_json_default, indent=2)
              print()
          return 0

- sfind-73b866f942f24d6e8f842b6cf353943a: Checkpoint record carries no integrity check and CKPRST restart routines are empty [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:21 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    The checkpoint VSAM record stores 400 bytes of opaque restart state with no checksum, hash or verification field, and the CKPRST module that uses this copybook implements init/take/commit/restart as empty paragraphs, so restart cannot resume from a verified state.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SI-7/SI-7(1) (integrity checks on information at security-relevant transitional states) and SC-24 (fail to a known state preserving state information) for the batch checkpoint/restart framework.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    CKPRST.cpy 52-56 defines CHECKPOINT-RECORD as key plus CKR-DATA X(400) with no integrity field; 6-47 define the control area (last key, counters, restart mode) with nothing verifying them. CKPRST.cbl 43-57 contains only comments in PROC-INIT, PROC-TAKE-CHECKPOINT, PROC-COMMIT-CHECKPOINT and PROC-RESTART.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Restarted batch runs (BCHCTL00, PRCSEQ00, RCVPRC00, HISTLD00 copy CKPRST) have no verified restart point; a corrupted or altered checkpoint is accepted and can cause duplicate or skipped position/history updates.
    - No record of checkpoint commits exists to support recovery.
    - Not verified: whether production uses a different checkpoint implementation.
  Recommendation: Add a CKR-CHECKSUM (e.g. SHA-256 via ICSF over CKR-KEY+CKR-DATA) written at PROC-TAKE/COMMIT and verified in PROC-RESTART, failing the job if it mismatches; implement the four CKPRST paragraphs (write/rewrite/read of CHECKPOINT-FILE with file-status checks).

    Code:
      def test_suite_copybooks_poswrt_parity(tmp_path):
          """The repo's own POSREC/TRNREC (COMP-3 money fields) written by COBOL, read by Python."""
          exe = compile_cobol(COBOL_FIXTURES / "POSWRT.cbl", tmp_path, "ibm")
          run_cobol(exe, tmp_path)
          pos = load_layout(COPYBOOKS / "common" / "POSREC.cpy", dialect=GNUCOBOL_IBM)
          trn = load_layout(COPYBOOKS / "common" / "TRNREC.cpy", dialect=GNUCOBOL_IBM)
          p = [pos.decode(r) for r in iter_records((tmp_path / "positions.dat").read_bytes(), pos.length)]
          t = [trn.decode(r) for r in iter_records((tmp_path / "transactions.dat").read_bytes(), trn.length)]
          assert [r["POS-QUANTITY"] for r in p] == [Decimal("1234567.8912"), Decimal("99999999999.9999")]
          assert p[0]["POS-COST-BASIS"] == Decimal("-98765432109.87")
          assert p[0]["POS-MARKET-VALUE"] == Decimal("0.01")
          assert p[1]["POS-MARKET-VALUE"] == Decimal("-9999999999999.99")
          assert (p[0]["POS-STATUS"], p[1]["POS-STATUS"]) == ("A", "C")
          # COBOL COMPUTE without ROUNDED truncates 100.5 * 19.99 = 2008.995 to the V99 scale.
          assert Decimal("100.5") * Decimal("19.99") == Decimal("2008.995")
          assert t[0]["TRN-AMOUNT"] == Decimal("2008.99")
          assert t[1]["TRN-AMOUNT"] == Decimal("-12.50")
          assert t[1]["TRN-TYPE"] == "FE" and t[1]["TRN-STATUS"] == "R"
          # Python port writes the same records -> byte-identical -> diff harness reports MATCH.
          again = b"".join(pos.encode(r) for r in p)
          assert again == (tmp_path / "positions.dat").read_bytes()
          assert diff_bytes(pos, (tmp_path / "positions.dat").read_bytes(), again).ok
  Snippet: python/tests/conftest.py:16-38
    Commentary: cobc located via shutil.which and invoked with argument lists.
    Code:
      COBC = shutil.which("cobc")
      requires_cobc = pytest.mark.skipif(COBC is None, reason="GnuCOBOL (cobc) not installed")
      
      
      def copybook_includes() -> list[str]:
          dirs = [COBOL_FIXTURES] + [d for d in COPYBOOKS.iterdir() if d.is_dir()]
          return [f"-I{d}" for d in dirs]
      
      
      def compile_cobol(source: Path, workdir: Path, std: str = "default", extra: list[str] = ()) -> Path:
          exe = workdir / source.stem.lower()
          cmd = [COBC, "-x", "-w", f"-std={std}", *extra, *copybook_includes(), "-o", str(exe), str(source)]
          r = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
          if r.returncode:
              raise RuntimeError(f"cobc failed: {' '.join(cmd)}\n{r.stdout}\n{r.stderr}")
          return exe
      
      
      def run_cobol(exe: Path, workdir: Path) -> str:
          r = subprocess.run([str(exe)], cwd=workdir, capture_output=True, text=True)
          if r.returncode:
              raise RuntimeError(f"{exe.name} failed rc={r.returncode}\n{r.stdout}\n{r.stderr}")
          return r.stdout

  Description: ##### Overview
    
    The job unconditionally deletes the PORTFOLIO.MASTER.FILE cluster and resets MAXCC to 0, then the DEFINE CLUSTER statements follow a `//*` line that ends the in-stream SYSIN, so they are not passed to IDCAMS; the job can leave the master deleted with a clean return code.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-24 (fail to a known state) and CM-6 (documented, correct configuration of the master data store).
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    PORTDEF.jcl 18-22: IDCAMS SYSIN with DELETE and SET MAXCC = 0. Line 23 `//*` in columns 1-2 terminates the DD * data, so 27-33 (DEFINE CLUSTER) are parsed as JCL, not IDCAMS input.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Running this job destroys the master file and may not recreate it, while reporting success.
    - No audit record of the deletion.
    - Inferred from standard JES in-stream rules; validate by submitting in a test LPAR.
  Recommendation: Put the DEFINE CLUSTER statements inside the SYSIN stream (remove the `//*` lines between DELETE and DEFINE), use `IF LASTCC = 8 THEN SET MAXCC = 0` only for the not-found case, and require explicit approval before delete.

    Commentary: load_layout reads with errors=replace and rejects missing/ambiguous records.
    Code:
          """Load one record layout from a copybook file.
      
          ``record`` selects the 01-level by name when the copybook defines several
          (e.g. DBTBLS.cpy); otherwise the copybook must contain exactly one.
          """
          layouts = parse_copybook(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"), dialect)
          if record:
              for l in layouts:
                  if l.name == record.upper():
                      return l
              raise KeyError(f"{path}: no 01-level named {record}; have {[l.name for l in layouts]}")
          if len(layouts) != 1:
              raise ValueError(f"{path}: defines {len(layouts)} records {[l.name for l in layouts]}; pass record=")
          return layouts[0]

- sfind-eededaeaead042ce9fb094acf5f198db: PORTMSTR create and delete leave no audit record; update audit broken [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:12 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTMSTR creates (`WRITE`), updates (`REWRITE`) and deletes (`DELETE`) portfolio master records. Only the update path calls the audit routine. `2100-LOG-PORTFOLIO-UPDATE` refers to data items PORTMSTR never defines and records no real before-image, and the AUDPROC return code is ignored.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2/AU-12 require audit records for the defined events. Create, update and delete of the authoritative portfolio master are the core audited events in this system (the AUDITLOG layout defines CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE actions). AU-3 requires the record content, and AU-5 requires a response when logging fails.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 115-136 (create, `WRITE` at 126) and 209-226 (delete, `DELETE` at 215) contain no `CALL 'AUDPROC'`.
    - Line 206: update performs `2100-LOG-PORTFOLIO-UPDATE`. In that paragraph (lines 268-288), `LS-AUDIT-REQUEST`, `LS-SYSTEM-ID`, `USERID`, `TERMINAL-ID`, `WS-BEFORE-IMAGE`, `PORT-ACCOUNT-NO`, `PORT-RECORD` and the other `LS-*` targets are not declared in PORTMSTR's WORKING-STORAGE or LINKAGE (lines 41-80). The before-image is never captured before the `REWRITE` at 194.
    - Line 287: the call result (`LS-RETURN-CODE` in AUDPROC) is never checked.
    - Lines 243-263 duplicate the `2100-` paragraph prefix with further undefined items, which suggests template code that was never integrated.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Portfolio creation and deletion, including irreversible removal of master records, leave no trace of who did what.
    - Updates cannot produce a valid before/after record, and failed audit writes go unnoticed.
    - Assumption: callers of PORTMSTR do not write their own audit records. None was found in this batch.
  Recommendation: Declare the AUDPROC request area (matching AUDPROC's LS-AUDIT-REQUEST) and a before-image buffer in PORTMSTR. READ the existing record before REWRITE/DELETE to capture the before-image, obtain the user and terminal IDs from the caller or the execution environment, and call AUDPROC with action CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE and SUCC/FAIL status on every mutation path, failures included. Check the AUDPROC return code and fail the operation (or raise a critical error) when the audit write fails.
  Note: Merged duplicate: sfind-11f0ed3759764d2f8950c1672c79ed65. Member of batch audit group sfind-8c392cb4ee2143e3907add782866d6eb.
  Code owners: Sergio DuBois <sergio.dubois@gmail.com>
  Scan session: 0f683593048c40b6828dc363f5984ec6
  Snippet: src/programs/portfolio/PORTMSTR.cbl:115-136
    Commentary: The create path writes the master record with no audit call.

                 MOVE LS-PORTFOLIO TO PORTFOLIO-RECORD

                 IF WS-RETURN-CODE NOT = WS-SUCCESS

- sfind-e55c6680f9844fbc92bd3a4cd21e2f22: Denied or failed inquiry access attempts are never written to AUDITLOG [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:12 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    INQONLN asks SECMGR to write an access record (`SEC-REQUEST-TYPE 'L'`) only when both validation and authorization succeed. Denied and failed attempts leave no audit record. When the audit write itself fails, the data has already been displayed, and the only response is an error routine and RETURN.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2/AU-12 call for audit records of the defined event types, and access-control decisions (successful and unsuccessful) are standard ones. AU-5 requires a response to audit logging failures. INQONLN is the code that decides which online access events get recorded.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 151-169: the `'L'` log request sits inside two nested `IF SEC-RESPONSE-CODE = 0` blocks. A non-zero result from validate (`'V'`) or authorize (`'A'`) skips logging entirely.
    - Lines 82-88: the only handling for a non-zero code is `P900-ERROR-ROUTINE` (ERRHNDL, a warning) plus RETURN. These lines also cover an audit failure ('Audit logging failed' from SECMGR), which arrives after the inquiry output was sent.
    
    ##### Impact
    - No record exists of unauthorized attempts to view portfolio or history data, so security reviews of failed access are impossible.
    - An audit-write failure does not stop disclosure and raises no alert beyond the ERRHNDL warning.
    - Not verified: whether ERRHNDL's DB2 error log is reviewed or alerted on (it logs the message at warning severity).
  Recommendation: Call SECMGR 'L' on every decision, passing an outcome indicator (granted, denied validation, denied authorization) so AUDITLOG records failed attempts too. Do the log write before the inquiry, and when the audit write fails, deny the inquiry and raise an ERR-ABEND/ERR-CRITICAL condition through ERRHNDL instead of a warning.
  Note: Member of root-cause group sfind-e434e488f3df422eb607fbdf6650e294 (SECMGR never audits denials). Merged duplicates: sfind-326ea7fc06a94a7e9af723c16e8c0b6f, sfind-a139bc1ca7ce498c8dd21d288baf7934.

- sfind-d577c42c52b546d0bdc74ad2814b79a2: INQONLN dispatches portfolio and history inquiries before running SECMGR checks [high/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:12 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    In each loop of `P100-PROCESS-REQUEST`, INQONLN runs the requested function (LINK to INQPORT/INQHIST, which read and SEND position/history data) and only afterwards calls `P050-SECURITY-CHECK`. The SECMGR validate/authorize decision therefore cannot stop the disclosure it is meant to gate. Any CICS transaction-level security on PINQ (RACF/ESM, outside this repo) is not visible here.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires that approved authorizations are enforced before logical access to information is granted. INQONLN is the only entry program of transaction PINQ (`src/cics/PORTDFN.csd`) and is where the application's own authorization (SECMGR resource `INQONLN`/`READ`) is invoked. Signon and transaction-attach security are handled by CICS/ESM configuration outside the repo.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 62-77: the EVALUATE on `WS-COMMAREA-FUNCTION` performs `P300-PORTFOLIO-INQUIRY` / `P400-HISTORY-INQUIRY` right away.
    - Lines 79-88: `P050-SECURITY-CHECK` runs only after that, and a non-zero `SEC-RESPONSE-CODE` causes a RETURN once the data has already been sent.
    - Lines 101-117: the LINKs pass the terminal-supplied COMMAREA (account number) to INQPORT/INQHIST, which display the data (INQPORT line 46-52 SEND MAP).
    
    ```
    RECEIVE MAP --> LINK INQPORT/INQHIST --> SEND MAP (data shown) --> SECMGR V/A --> deny => RETURN
    ```
    
    ##### Impact
    - Portfolio positions and transaction history for any account number typed on the screen are shown to users who would fail the SECMGR `AUTHFILE` check.
    - The application-level access decision has no effect on disclosure. Only ESM transaction security (not verifiable here) gates PINQ.
    - Assumption: INQPORT/INQHIST do no checks of their own. I confirmed this for INQPORT, and INQHIST has no SECMGR call.
  Recommendation: Run P050-SECURITY-CHECK right after RECEIVE MAP and before the EVALUATE dispatch. Perform the INQP/INQH branches only when validate and authorize both return 0, and on denial send a generic error map and stop without LINKing to INQPORT/INQHIST. Initialize WS-SECURITY-REQUEST (including SEC-RESPONSE-CODE) before each check.

- sfind-b7d4a1d950ce437e835788ef10c7b8fb: INQPORT returns positions for any COMMAREA account number without ownership check [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:12 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    INQPORT reads POSFILE using the account number from the COMMAREA, which INQONLN fills straight from the terminal map, and sends the record to the screen. Nothing checks whether the signed-on user may see that account. The only application authorization in the flow (SECMGR) works at program level (`INQONLN`/`READ`) and runs after INQPORT, per the INQONLN finding.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires access to information to follow approved authorizations, and AC-6 requires access limited to what the user needs. Client portfolio positions are per-account data, and INQPORT is the component that reads and discloses them. Record-level dataset security in the ESM (outside the repo) cannot restrict by account inside one VSAM file.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 58-59: the COMMAREA is copied in as-is.
    - Lines 63-70: `INQCOM-ACCOUNT-NO` becomes the RIDFLD for `READ FILE('POSFILE')`. There is no format check and no lookup of the user's entitlements.
    - Lines 80-84: the record is sent to the terminal.
    - SECMGR lines 57-67 (P200) authorize on `RESOURCE = 'INQONLN'` only, never on the account.
    
    ##### Impact
    - Any user with access to PINQ can list another client's positions (units, cost basis, market value) by entering that client's account number.
    - Assumption: no ESM or account-level entitlement table outside the repo is consulted. Nothing in the repo references one.
  Recommendation: Before the POSFILE read, validate the account number's format, then authorize the signed-on user (EXEC CICS ASSIGN USERID) for that specific account. Do this through a SECMGR 'A' request with the account as the resource, an entitlement table, or EXEC CICS QUERY SECURITY on an account-level profile. Return a generic not-authorized message on denial and log the decision.

- sfind-8c392cb4ee2143e3907add782866d6eb: PORTUPDT batch REWRITEs portfolio master without any audit record [high/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:12 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    PORTUPDT applies sequential update transactions (status, client name, total value) to the portfolio master with `REWRITE`. It writes no audit record and does not update the record's own `PORT-LAST-USER`/`PORT-LAST-MAINT` fields, so each change leaves no who/what/before/after trace.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AU-2/AU-12 require audit records for the defined event types, and AU-3 requires their content. Changes to the authoritative portfolio master, including client name and holdings value, are audited event types per the AUDITLOG UPDATE action, and AUDPROC exists for this purpose.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 49-69: `2200-APPLY-UPDATE` overwrites fields and `REWRITE`s with no AUDPROC call and no before-image saved.
    - The only output is SYSOUT counters (lines 77-78).

    ##### Impact
    - Bulk changes to client names, account status and valuations are untraceable to a job, a user, or prior values.
    - Assumption: no external SMF/ESM dataset-level logging is relied on for record-level change history. That would record dataset access only, not field changes.
  Recommendation: Before each REWRITE, copy the read record to a before-image. After the REWRITE, call AUDPROC with system/user (job/RACF user), program PORTUPDT, action UPDATE, SUCC/FAIL status, the PORT-KEY, and the before/after images. Set PORT-LAST-USER/PORT-LAST-MAINT, check AUDPROC's return code, and stop with a non-zero RC if auditing fails.

- sfind-7d7afa4bffee4818ae0ead8967ed264d: SECMGR authorization stores COUNT(*) into SQLCA group and compares group [medium/dismissed] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:08:12 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    `P200-CHECK-AUTH` selects `COUNT(*)` INTO `:WS-DB2-AREA`, the 01-level group that holds the SQLCA, and then makes the grant decision with `IF WS-DB2-AREA > 0`. That is an alphanumeric group comparison, not a test of an integer count. The access decision does not reliably reflect AUTHFILE contents and may fail open. The exact runtime result depends on precompiler/compiler handling, which I could not run.
    
    ##### Applicability
    AC-3 requires enforcement of approved authorizations. SECMGR is the application's central access-decision routine for the online inquiry programs.
    
    ##### Evidence
    - Lines 1-3 of the working storage (file lines 14-15): `WS-DB2-AREA` contains only `INCLUDE SQLCA`.
    - Lines 78-85: the COUNT is written into that group, so the SQLCA itself is overlaid.
    - Lines 87-95: `WS-DB2-AREA > 0` compares the group, which starts with SQLCAID text, against zero as alphanumeric. A non-blank character string compares greater than '0', so any `SQLCODE = 0` result can come out as a grant whatever the count is.
    - Lines 41-51: the EVALUATE has no `WHEN OTHER`, so an unrecognised request type returns with whatever `SEC-RESPONSE-CODE` the caller passed in (INQONLN never initializes it).
    
    ##### Impact
    - Users with no AUTHFILE row may be authorized for `INQONLN READ`.
    - Unknown request types are not denied by default.
    - Not verified: actual compiled behaviour. Validation should test a user absent from AUTHFILE.
  Recommendation: Declare a dedicated `WS-AUTH-COUNT PIC S9(9) COMP` host variable, select COUNT(*) INTO it, and grant only when `SQLCODE = 0 AND WS-AUTH-COUNT > 0`. Add `WHEN OTHER` that sets SEC-RESPONSE-CODE to a deny value, and initialize SEC-RESPONSE-CODE to non-zero at entry so all paths fail closed.

  Snippet: src/programs/online/SECMGR.cbl:77-102
    Commentary: COUNT(*) goes into the SQLCA group, and the grant is decided by a group comparison.
    Code:
                 
             P200-CHECK-AUTH.
                 EXEC SQL
                      SELECT COUNT(*)
                      INTO :WS-DB2-AREA
                      FROM AUTHFILE
                      WHERE USER_ID = :SEC-USER-ID
                        AND RESOURCE = :SEC-RESOURCE-NAME
                        AND ACCESS_TYPE = :SEC-ACCESS-TYPE
                 END-EXEC.
                 
                 EVALUATE SQLCODE
                     WHEN 0
                          IF WS-DB2-AREA > 0
                             MOVE 0 TO SEC-RESPONSE-CODE
                          ELSE
                             MOVE 'Access denied' 
                               TO SEC-ERROR-INFO
                             MOVE 8 TO SEC-RESPONSE-CODE
                          END-IF
                     WHEN OTHER
                          MOVE 'Authorization check failed' 
                            TO SEC-ERROR-INFO
                          MOVE 12 TO SEC-RESPONSE-CODE
                 END-EVALUATE.
             P200-EXIT.
  Snippet: src/programs/online/SECMGR.cbl:40-51

    `P300-LOG-ACCESS` inserts timestamp, user, terminal, transaction, program and access type into AUDITLOG. It records no event outcome (success/failure) and no identity of the object accessed (account number). Because it is only called after a grant, it also cannot tell success from failure.

    - Assumption: the DB2 AUDITLOG table has, or can be given, status and key columns. The DDL was not part of this batch.
