- sfind-a8900dd4b935450dbcc4ae3945941c2c: DISCONNECT-FROM-DB2 commits unconditionally regardless of unit-of-work state [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:07:43 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    The shared disconnect paragraph issues COMMIT WORK with no check of SQLCODE or caller status, and CONNECT failure only rolls back and logs without stopping the caller. Callers that reach disconnect after an unchecked failure will commit partial work.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-24 (fail in a known state) and SI-7 (information integrity) apply to the common DB2 procedures copied into DB2CONN, DB2CMT, DB2ERR, DB2STAT and HISTLD00.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    DBPROC.cpy lines 36-43: COMMIT WORK then CONNECT RESET, with no condition and no SQLCODE check on the COMMIT. Lines 26-33: on connect failure, DB2-ERROR-ROUTINE runs but control returns to the caller with no abort flag set. Lines 46-57 roll back only when a caller invokes CHECK-SQL-STATUS.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Partial updates to DB2 history/position tables can be committed on an error path, diverging from VSAM.
    - A failed COMMIT is silently ignored.
    - Did not trace every caller to confirm one actually reaches disconnect after an unhandled error.
  Recommendation: Commit only when a success flag set by the caller is true (otherwise ROLLBACK), check SQLCODE after COMMIT and route failures to DB2-ERROR-ROUTINE, and set a fatal return code on connect failure so callers terminate.
  Note: Same pattern (disconnect issues unconditional COMMIT) in DB2CONN.cbl 2000-DISCONNECT sfind-dbd2209d80d34c0ba1764351f552245c, which has its own copy of the logic; DBPROC.cpy is COPYed into DB2ERR, DB2STAT, DB2CMT, DB2CONN and HISTLD00. Fix both: commit only on a known-good unit of work, otherwise ROLLBACK.
  Code owners: Sergio DuBois <sergio.dubois@gmail.com>

- sfind-8fc5dbc778bd4c6daa1e2bffa2ffd465: PORTPLAN bind uses wildcard PKLIST, RETAIN, VALIDATE(RUN), no OWNER [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:07:43 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    The DB2 plan used by the online system binds any package in collection-wildcard `*.PORTPKG.*`, retains existing EXECUTE grants on replace, and defers authorization checks to run time, with no OWNER or QUALIFIER specified.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    CM-6 (documented, restrictive configuration settings) and AC-6 (least privilege) apply to the plan executed by CICS DB2ENTRY PORTDB2. DB2 GRANTs themselves are outside the repo.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    PORTPLAN.sql line 2 binds PKLIST(*.PORTPKG.*), lines 3-4 ACTION(REPLACE) RETAIN, line 5 VALIDATE(RUN); there is no OWNER/QUALIFIER clause, so ownership defaults to the binder and unqualified table names resolve to the binder's ID.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Any package bound into a PORTPKG collection on any location becomes executable under the plan's authority.
    - Missing-privilege errors surface only at run time rather than blocking the bind.
    - Plan ownership depends on who ran the bind, not a controlled ID.
  Recommendation: Bind with an explicit PKLIST(location.PORTPKG.pkgname,...) or at least a fixed location, add OWNER(<controlled authid>) and QUALIFIER(<schema>), use VALIDATE(BIND), and review RETAIN so EXECUTE grants are re-approved on replace.
  Code owners: Sergio DuBois <sergio.dubois@gmail.com>
  Scan session: 0f683593048c40b6828dc363f5984ec6

    Commentary: Subprocess harness and fixture.
    Code:
      def compile_cobol(source: Path, workdir: Path, std: str = "default", extra: list[str] = ()) -> Path:
          exe = workdir / source.stem.lower()
          cmd = [COBC, "-x", "-w", f"-std={std}", *extra, *copybook_includes(), "-o", str(exe), str(source)]
          r = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
          if r.returncode:
              raise RuntimeError(f"cobc failed: {' '.join(cmd)}\n{r.stdout}\n{r.stderr}")
          return exe
      
      
      def run_cobol(exe: Path, workdir: Path) -> str:
          r = subprocess.run([str(exe)], cwd=workdir, capture_output=True, text=True)
          if r.returncode:
              raise RuntimeError(f"{exe.name} failed rc={r.returncode}\n{r.stdout}\n{r.stderr}")
          return r.stdout
      
      
      @pytest.fixture
      def cobol_build(tmp_path):
          """Compile a COBOL fixture into tmp_path and return (exe, workdir)."""
          def _build(name: str, std: str = "default", extra: list[str] = ()):
              return compile_cobol(COBOL_FIXTURES / name, tmp_path, std, extra), tmp_path
          return _build

    ##### Applicability

- sfind-1de0d118e69b4b91a08bc1492431ed2d: SECMGR security program shares USER key and group with application programs [medium/open] repo: COG-GTM/COBOL-Legacy-Benchmark-Suite, scan: scan-ac0861710f1d401ca6912252566f41bb created 2026-11-04 09:07:43 UTC, category: deviation
  Description: ##### Overview
    
    SECMGR, which performs user validation, AUTHFILE authorization checks and AUDITLOG writes, is defined identically to ordinary inquiry programs (EXECKEY(USER), same group), and the PINQ transaction carries no resource/command security attributes. Region-level storage protection (SIT STGPROT/TRANISO) is outside the repo and may mitigate this.
    
    ##### Applicability
    
    SC-3 requires isolating security functions from non-security functions; AC-3/AC-6(1) require enforced access to security functions. The CSD is the in-repo definition of these programs and transactions; SIT parameters and RACF XTRAN/XRES classes must come from region configuration.
    
    ##### Evidence
    
    PORTDFN.csd lines 56-61 define SECMGR with EXECKEY(USER) like INQONLN/INQPORT etc. (lines 14-54). PINQ (lines 6-11) specifies no RESSEC(YES) or CMDSEC(YES). SECMGR.cbl lines 41-52 show it performs validation, authorization and audit for callers that LINK to it with a caller-supplied COMMAREA.
    
    ##### Impact
    
    - Security decision and audit logic is not separated by key or definition from user programs; any user-key program can overwrite its storage if storage protection is not enabled.
    - CICS-level resource checks are not requested, so access enforcement relies entirely on application LINKs to SECMGR.
    - Could not verify STGPROT/TRANISO/XRES settings of the region.
  Recommendation: Define SECMGR with EXECKEY(CICS) (or in a separately controlled group), add RESSEC(YES) and CMDSEC(YES) to PINQ, and document required SIT STGPROT=YES/TRANISO=YES for the region.
  Note: Merged duplicate: sfind-807b461c1bc146a7a3eef9cc641795e6. Partly depends on region SIT STGPROT/TRANISO and RACF XTRAN/XRES (platform, out of scope).

  Scan session: 0f683593048c40b6828dc363f5984ec6
  Snippet: python/cobol_fields/fields.py:355-441
    Commentary: Decode validation and error messages.
    Code:
              With ``strict`` the decoder rejects invalid sign nibbles / zones and
              non-digit nibbles, which is how corrupted or mis-aligned data shows up.
              """
              if len(raw) != self.length:
                  raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: expected {self.length} bytes, got {len(raw)}")
              enc = encoding or self.dialect.encoding
              u = self.usage
              if u is Usage.DISPLAY and self.pic.kind is not PicKind.NUMERIC:
                  return raw.decode(enc)
              if u is Usage.COMP_X:
                  return self._scaled(int.from_bytes(raw, "big", signed=False))
              if u in (Usage.COMP, Usage.COMP_5):
                  n = int.from_bytes(raw, self._byteorder, signed=self.signed)
                  if strict and u is Usage.COMP and self.dialect.binary_truncate and abs(n) > self.pic.max_value:
                      raise CobolFieldError(
                          f"{self.name}: binary value {n} exceeds PIC {self.pic.raw} (TRUNC(BIN)/COMP-5 data?)")
                  return self._scaled(n)
              if u is Usage.COMP_3:
                  return self._scaled(self._decode_packed(raw, strict))
              return self._scaled(self._decode_zoned(raw, enc, strict))
      
          def _decode_packed(self, raw: bytes, strict: bool) -> int:
              nibbles = []
              for b in raw:
                  nibbles.append(b >> 4)
                  nibbles.append(b & 0x0F)
              sign = nibbles.pop()
              if sign in (0xD, 0xB):
                  neg = True
              elif sign in (0xC, 0xF, 0xA, 0xE):
                  neg = False
              elif strict:
                  raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: invalid packed sign nibble 0x{sign:X} in {raw.hex()}")
              else:
                  neg = False
              if len(nibbles) > self.pic.digits:          # even digit count: leading pad nibble
                  pad, nibbles = nibbles[0], nibbles[1:]
                  if strict and pad != 0:
                      raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: non-zero pad nibble 0x{pad:X} in {raw.hex()}")
              if strict and any(d > 9 for d in nibbles):
                  raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: invalid packed digit in {raw.hex()}")
              n = 0
              for d in nibbles:
                  n = n * 10 + (d if d <= 9 else 0)
              return -n if neg else n
      
          def _decode_zoned(self, raw: bytes, enc: str, strict: bool) -> int:
              ebcdic = _is_ebcdic(enc)
              neg = False
              if self.sign.separate and self.signed:
                  s_idx = 0 if self.sign is SignMode.LEADING_SEPARATE else -1
                  s = raw[s_idx:s_idx + 1].decode(enc) if s_idx == 0 else raw[-1:].decode(enc)
                  if s not in "+-":
                      raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: invalid separate sign {s!r}")
                  neg = s == "-"
                  body = raw[1:] if s_idx == 0 else raw[:-1]
                  text = body.decode(enc)
              else:
                  idx = 0 if self.sign is SignMode.LEADING else len(raw) - 1
                  buf = bytearray(raw)
                  b = buf[idx]
                  zone, digit = b >> 4, b & 0x0F
                  if ebcdic:
                      if zone == _EBCDIC_NEG:
                          neg = True
                      elif zone in (_EBCDIC_POS, _EBCDIC_UNSIGNED):
                          pass
                      elif strict:
                          raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: invalid EBCDIC sign zone 0x{zone:X}")
                      buf[idx] = 0xF0 | digit
                      text = bytes(buf).decode(enc)
                  else:
                      ch = chr(b)
                      if zone == _ASCII_NEG:
                          neg = True
                          buf[idx] = 0x30 | digit
                      elif ch in _OVERPUNCH_POS:
                          buf[idx] = 0x30 | _OVERPUNCH_POS[ch]
                      elif ch in _OVERPUNCH_NEG:
                          neg = True
                          buf[idx] = 0x30 | _OVERPUNCH_NEG[ch]
                      elif zone != _ASCII_POS and strict:
                          raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: invalid ASCII sign byte 0x{b:02X}")
                      text = bytes(buf).decode(enc)
              if strict and not text.isdigit():
                  raise CobolFieldError(f"{self.name}: non-numeric DISPLAY data {text!r}")
              n = int(text) if text.strip() else 0
